+  /* Phase 6: HTML export. A standalone, offline-openable .html file that reproduces the stage ??+   * absolute positioning, colours, rotation, DOM order (== stacking order, see the comment on

+   * Deliberately does NOT fetch or inline style.css: the exported file must stand on its own, and

+   * govern how a `.el` is positioned and painted (see style.css's own "CONTRACT" comments) ??the
+   * SAME rules, copied rather than derived, because this file has no build step to share them with.

+    '.el .txt[data-align="CENTER"]{justify-content:center;text-align:center}',
+    '.el .txt[data-align="RIGHT"]{justify-content:flex-end;text-align:right}',
+    '.el .media{position:absolute;inset:0;width:100%;height:100%;object-fit:fill}',
+  ].join('\n');
+
+  // Removes everything about a cloned `.el` that exists only for the EDITOR's benefit: selection/
+  // multi-select/blocked-flash outlines, the resize/rotate/connector-endpoint handles, and a
+  // lingering contenteditable left on `.txt .body` (only present mid-edit, but defended anyway).
+  function stripEditorChrome(clone) {
+    clone.classList.remove('selected', 'editing', 'multi-selected', 'blocked');
+    var handles = clone.querySelectorAll('.handle');
+    for (var i = 0; i < handles.length; i++) handles[i].remove();
+    var body = clone.querySelector('.txt .body');
+    if (body) body.removeAttribute('contenteditable');
+  }
+
+  /* Phase 6 REPAIR: a File -> its `data:<mime>;base64,...` URL, unlike fileToBase64() below (which
+   * strips that prefix for the server's pure-base64 field) -- an exported <img>/<video> `src` wants
+   * the prefix so the browser opening the standalone file knows how to decode it. */
+  function fileToDataURL(file) {
+    return new Promise(function (resolve, reject) {
+      var reader = new FileReader();
+      reader.onload = function () { resolve(String(reader.result || '')); };
+      reader.onerror = function () { reject(reader.error || new Error('could not read the file')); };
+      reader.readAsDataURL(file);
+    });
+  }
+
+  /* Phase 6 REPAIR (Codex Critic BLOCK): window.uploadedMedia[src].url is an object URL --
+   * URL.createObjectURL(file) -- valid only inside THIS TAB'S SESSION. renderEl() binds it onto the
+   * live `.media` element for on-stage preview, which is fine there because the tab stays open; but
+   * exportElementHTML() below clones that live node, and a blob: URL baked into a downloaded HTML
+   * file dies the moment this tab closes (or immediately in any OTHER browser/tab), breaking every
+   * image/video in a file whose whole point is to keep working offline and standalone. Converts the
+   * SAME File object the PPTX export already multipart-uploads (window.uploadedMedia[src].file) to
+   * a data: URL instead, so the bytes travel inside the .html file itself. Cached on the entry so
+   * exporting twice (or two elements sharing one `src`) reads the File once.
+   */
+  function mediaDataURL(src) {
+    var entry = window.uploadedMedia && window.uploadedMedia[src];
+    if (!entry) return Promise.resolve('');
+    if (entry.dataUrl) return Promise.resolve(entry.dataUrl);
+    return fileToDataURL(entry.file).then(function (url) {
+      entry.dataUrl = url;
+      return url;
+    });
+  }
+
+  /* Phase 6 REPAIR (Codex Critic BLOCK): an ALLOWLIST, not a denylist. `javascript:`/`data:`/
+   * `vbscript:` etc. are refused by virtue of not being http/https/mailto -- not by name-matching
+   * every dangerous scheme there is, which is a list that only ever grows. The scheme regex below
+   * requires the whole run of characters up to the first ':' to be letters/digits/+/./- (RFC 3986
+   * scheme syntax) -- a control character or a space ANYWHERE in that run (e.g. a tab smuggled into
+   * "java\tscript:", which some browsers strip before reading the scheme) breaks the match and
+   * falls through to the "not one of the allowed forms" rejection below, so a browser-side strip-
+   * then-reinterpret trick cannot turn a rejected string into an accepted one. Returns '' (== "no
+   * link") for anything that is not http:, https:, mailto:, or an in-page anchor ('#...') --
+   * including a bare scheme-less string like "example.com", which is not one of the allowed forms
+   * either.
+   */
+  function sanitizeLink(link) {
+    var v = String(link || '').trim();
+    if (!v) return '';
+    if (v.charAt(0) === '#') return v;
+    var m = /^([a-zA-Z][a-zA-Z0-9+.-]*):/.exec(v);
+    if (!m) return '';
+    var scheme = m[1].toLowerCase();
+    return (scheme === 'http' || scheme === 'https' || scheme === 'mailto') ? v : '';
+  }
+
+  // Clones the LIVE, already-rendered `.el` node rather than re-deriving markup from state a second
+  // time: geometry/colour/rotation are already correct inline styles (renderEl()), so the exported
+  // file cannot silently drift from what the user is looking at. `display: contents` makes the <a>
+  // generate no box of its own, so the wrapped element's own absolute positioning keeps working
+  // completely unmodified and DOM order is unaffected. `dataUrls` is a {src: data-url} map the
+  // caller has already resolved (mediaDataURL is async; this function is not, so it cannot do that
+  // resolution itself) -- a media element's cloned `src` attribute (still the transient blob: URL
+  // renderEl() bound onto the live node) is overwritten with the looked-up data: URL before
+  // serializing. A shape/textbox's `link` (never set on a connector or media element -- read() only
+  // populates it on those two branches) is passed through sanitizeLink() before it is allowed into
+  // an `href` attribute; esc() on top of that guards against attribute-injection via quote
+  // characters, exactly as it already did before this repair.
+  function exportElementHTML(el, dataUrls) {
+    var clone = el.cloneNode(true);
+    stripEditorChrome(clone);
+    var type = el.dataset.type;
+    if (isMediaType(type)) {
+      var media = clone.querySelector('.media');
+      if (media) media.setAttribute('src', dataUrls[el.dataset.src] || '');
+      return clone.outerHTML;
+    }
+    var link = sanitizeLink(read(el).link);
+    return link
+      ? '<a href="' + esc(link) + '" style="display:contents;cursor:pointer">' + clone.outerHTML + '</a>'
+      : clone.outerHTML;
+  }
+
+  async function exportHTML() {
+    clearWarnings();
+    var elements = Array.prototype.slice.call(stage.querySelectorAll(':scope > .el'));
+
+    // Same missing-media guard $('b-export') already runs before the PPTX round trip: a reload
+    // clears window.uploadedMedia, so a src an on-stage element still names may have no File behind
+    // it any more.
+    var srcs = [];
+    elements.forEach(function (el) {
+      if (isMediaType(el.dataset.type) && srcs.indexOf(el.dataset.src) < 0) srcs.push(el.dataset.src);
+    });
+    var missing = srcs.filter(function (n) { return !window.uploadedMedia || !window.uploadedMedia[n]; });
+    if (missing.length) {
+      warn(
+        'Cannot export ??missing media file(s): ' + missing.join(', '),
+        'This element references an image/video this browser tab no longer has the bytes for ' +
+        '(a reload clears them). Re-add it with the Media buttons in the toolbar, or delete the ' +
+        'element, then export again.'
+      );
+      setStatus('Export failed.');
+      return;
+    }
+
+    setStatus('Exporting??);
+
+    var dataUrls;
+    try {
+      dataUrls = {};
+      for (var i = 0; i < srcs.length; i++) dataUrls[srcs[i]] = await mediaDataURL(srcs[i]);
+    } catch (e) {
+      warn('Could not embed a media file', String(e));
+      setStatus('Export failed.');
+      return;
+    }
+
+    // Named so the export still succeeds when a link is unsafe -- the ELEMENT is not dropped, only
+    // its href -- but the user is told why the exported file's link does not work, rather than
+    // silently shipping a slide that looks clickable and is not.
+    var blockedLinks = elements
+      .filter(function (el) { var link = read(el).link; return link && !sanitizeLink(link); })
+      .map(function (el) { return el.dataset.id; });
+    if (blockedLinks.length) {
+      warn(
+        'Some links were not exported: ' + blockedLinks.join(', '),
+        'Only http:, https:, mailto: links and in-page anchors (#...) are allowed in exported HTML.'
+      );
+    }
+
+    var body = elements.map(function (el) { return exportElementHTML(el, dataUrls); }).join('\n');
+    var html = '<!DOCTYPE html>\n<html lang="ko">\n<head>\n<meta charset="utf-8">\n' +
+      '<meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1">\n' +
+      '<title>' + esc(safeStem()) + '</title>\n<style>' + EXPORT_CSS + '</style>\n</head>\n<body>\n' +
+      '<div id="stage">\n<div id="title-field">' + titleField.innerHTML + '</div>\n' +
+      body + '\n</div>\n</body>\n</html>\n';
+    download(new Blob([html], { type: 'text/html' }), safeStem() + '.html');
+    setStatus('Downloaded HTML (' + elements.length + ' element(s)).');
+  }
+
+  $('b-export-html').addEventListener('click', exportHTML);

  650:   function esc(v) { return String(v).replace(/&/g, '&amp;').replace(/</g, '&lt;').replace(/"/g, '&quot;'); }
  651: 
  652:   // No fill => fill="none". No stroke => the stroke attribute is OMITTED ENTIRELY. Never
  653:   // stroke-width="0" to mean "no line": width 0 is a HAIRLINE in PowerPoint, not an absence.
  654:   function shapeMarkup(s) {
  655:     var fill = s.fill ? esc(s.fill) : 'none';
  656:     // Preview-only: SVG's fill-opacity is how this stage shows the alpha byte toIR() will fold
  657:     // into the fill's hex string. It never reaches the IR itself -- fill stays a plain 6-digit
  658:     // colour in dataset, and only toIR() combines it with fillAlpha at serialization time.
  659:     var fillOpacity = (s.fill && typeof s.fillAlpha === 'number' && s.fillAlpha < 100)
  660:       ? ' fill-opacity="' + (clamp(s.fillAlpha, 0, 100) / 100) + '"' : '';
  661:     var stroke = s.strokeColor
  662:       ? ' stroke="' + esc(s.strokeColor) + '" stroke-width="' + esc(s.strokeWidth) + '"'
  663:       : '';
  664:     var w = s.w, h = s.h;
  665:     switch (s.type) {
  666:       case 'rect':
  667:         return '<rect x="0" y="0" width="' + w + '" height="' + h + '" fill="' + fill + '"' + fillOpacity + stroke + '/>';
  668:       case 'ellipse':
  669:         return '<ellipse cx="' + w / 2 + '" cy="' + h / 2 + '" rx="' + w / 2 + '" ry="' + h / 2 +
  670:                '" fill="' + fill + '"' + fillOpacity + stroke + '/>';
  671:       case 'triangle':
  672:         // Apex top-centre, base along the bottom edge -- matches MSO_SHAPE.ISOSCELES_TRIANGLE.
  673:         return '<polygon points="' + w / 2 + ',0 ' + w + ',' + h + ' 0,' + h + '" fill="' + fill + '"' + fillOpacity + stroke + '/>';
  674:       case 'textbox':
  675:         // A textbox carries fill and stroke like any other shape; with neither, it draws nothing.
  676:         if (!s.fill && !s.strokeColor) return '';
  677:         return '<rect x="0" y="0" width="' + w + '" height="' + h + '" fill="' + fill + '"' + fillOpacity + stroke + '/>';
  678:       default:
  679:         return '';
  680:     }
  681:   }
  682: 
  683:   function connectorMarkup(s, ax, ay, bx, by) {
  684:     var mid = 'm-' + s.id;
  685:     // ONE marker, oriented auto-start-reverse, so it serves marker-start and marker-end both.
  686:     // refX sits on the tip (viewBox x = 10), so the tip lands ON the endpoint -- mirroring
  687:     // DrawingML's <a:headEnd> / <a:tailEnd>.

  690:       'markerWidth="6" markerHeight="6" orient="auto-start-reverse" markerUnits="strokeWidth">' +

  696: 
  697:     var attrs = 'fill="none" stroke="' + esc(s.color) + '" stroke-width="' + esc(s.width) + '"' + mk;
  698:

  810:       // multiple paragraphs stack instead of laying out side by side.

  814:       // keeping the caret out -- which meant the element was, to the keyboard and to every
  815:       // assistive technology, permanently editable, and editingText() could not tell "the caret is
  816:       // in the text" from "the text merely exists".
  817:       html += '<div class="txt"><div class="body"></div></div>';
  818:       for (var j = 0; j < HANDLES.length; j++) html += handleHTML(HANDLES[j]);
  819:       html += handleHTML('rot');
  820:     }
  821:     el.innerHTML = html;
  822:     write(el, s);
  823:     renderEl(el);
  824:     wire(el);
  825:     return el;
  826:   }

 2125:   // Removes everything about a cloned `.el` that exists only for the EDITOR's benefit: selection/
 2126:   // multi-select/blocked-flash outlines, the resize/rotate/connector-endpoint handles, and a
 2127:   // lingering contenteditable left on `.txt .body` (only present mid-edit, but defended anyway).
 2128:   function stripEditorChrome(clone) {
 2129:     clone.classList.remove('selected', 'editing', 'multi-selected', 'blocked');
 2130:     var handles = clone.querySelectorAll('.handle');
 2131:     for (var i = 0; i < handles.length; i++) handles[i].remove();

 2135: 
 2136:   /* Phase 6 REPAIR: a File -> its `data:<mime>;base64,...` URL, unlike fileToBase64() below (which
 2137:    * strips that prefix for the server's pure-base64 field) -- an exported <img>/<video> `src` wants
 2138:    * the prefix so the browser opening the standalone file knows how to decode it. */
 2139:   function fileToDataURL(file) {
 2140:     return new Promise(function (resolve, reject) {
 2141:       var reader = new FileReader();
 2142:       reader.onload = function () { resolve(String(reader.result || '')); };
 2143:       reader.onerror = function () { reject(reader.error || new Error('could not read the file')); };
 2144:       reader.readAsDataURL(file);
 2145:     });
 2146:   }
 2147: 
 2148:   /* Phase 6 REPAIR (Codex Critic BLOCK): window.uploadedMedia[src].url is an object URL --
 2149:    * URL.createObjectURL(file) -- valid only inside THIS TAB'S SESSION. renderEl() binds it onto the
 2150:    * live `.media` element for on-stage preview, which is fine there because the tab stays open; but
 2151:    * exportElementHTML() below clones that live node, and a blob: URL baked into a downloaded HTML
 2152:    * file dies the moment this tab closes (or immediately in any OTHER browser/tab), breaking every
 2153:    * image/video in a file whose whole point is to keep working offline and standalone. Converts the
 2154:    * SAME File object the PPTX export already multipart-uploads (window.uploadedMedia[src].file) to
 2155:    * a data: URL instead, so the bytes travel inside the .html file itself. Cached on the entry so
 2156:    * exporting twice (or two elements sharing one `src`) reads the File once.
 2157:    */
 2158:   function mediaDataURL(src) {
 2159:     var entry = window.uploadedMedia && window.uploadedMedia[src];
 2160:     if (!entry) return Promise.resolve('');
 2161:     if (entry.dataUrl) return Promise.resolve(entry.dataUrl);
 2162:     return fileToDataURL(entry.file).then(function (url) {
 2163:       entry.dataUrl = url;
 2164:       return url;
 2165:     });
 2166:   }
 2167: 
 2168:   /* Phase 6 REPAIR (Codex Critic BLOCK): an ALLOWLIST, not a denylist. `javascript:`/`data:`/
 2169:    * `vbscript:` etc. are refused by virtue of not being http/https/mailto -- not by name-matching
 2170:    * every dangerous scheme there is, which is a list that only ever grows. The scheme regex below
 2171:    * requires the whole run of characters up to the first ':' to be letters/digits/+/./- (RFC 3986
 2172:    * scheme syntax) -- a control character or a space ANYWHERE in that run (e.g. a tab smuggled into
 2173:    * "java\tscript:", which some browsers strip before reading the scheme) breaks the match and
 2174:    * falls through to the "not one of the allowed forms" rejection below, so a browser-side strip-
 2175:    * then-reinterpret trick cannot turn a rejected string into an accepted one. Returns '' (== "no
 2176:    * link") for anything that is not http:, https:, mailto:, or an in-page anchor ('#...') --
 2177:    * including a bare scheme-less string like "example.com", which is not one of the allowed forms
 2178:    * either.
 2179:    */
 2180:   function sanitizeLink(link) {
 2181:     var v = String(link || '').trim();
 2182:     if (!v) return '';
 2183:     if (v.charAt(0) === '#') return v;
 2184:     var m = /^([a-zA-Z][a-zA-Z0-9+.-]*):/.exec(v);
 2185:     if (!m) return '';
 2186:     var scheme = m[1].toLowerCase();
 2187:     return (scheme === 'http' || scheme === 'https' || scheme === 'mailto') ? v : '';
 2188:   }
 2189: 
 2190:   // Clones the LIVE, already-rendered `.el` node rather than re-deriving markup from state a second
 2191:   // time: geometry/colour/rotation are already correct inline styles (renderEl()), so the exported
 2192:   // file cannot silently drift from what the user is looking at. `display: contents` makes the <a>
 2193:   // generate no box of its own, so the wrapped element's own absolute positioning keeps working
 2194:   // completely unmodified and DOM order is unaffected. `dataUrls` is a {src: data-url} map the
 2195:   // caller has already resolved (mediaDataURL is async; this function is not, so it cannot do that
 2196:   // resolution itself) -- a media element's cloned `src` attribute (still the transient blob: URL
 2197:   // renderEl() bound onto the live node) is overwritten with the looked-up data: URL before

 2903:   /* Runs are hand-editable too. A paragraph with no `runs`, or a run with no `t`, is a file someone

 2907:   function paragraphsToHTML(paragraphs, defColor) {
 2908:     var list = Array.isArray(paragraphs) ? paragraphs : [];
 2909:     return list.map(function (p) {
 2910:       var runs = p && Array.isArray(p.runs) ? p.runs : [];

 2912:         if (!r || typeof r !== 'object') return '';

 2914:         var t = esc(raw).replace(/\n/g, '<br>');
 2915:         if (r.bold) t = '<b>' + t + '</b>';
 2916:         if (r.italic) t = '<i>' + t + '</i>';

 2918:         // reads the colour back off this span on the way out, so leaving it empty would round-trip
 2919:         // the colour as the string "undefined" and lose it.
 2920:         return '<span style="color: ' + esc(r.color || defColor) + '">' + t + '</span>';
 2921:       }).join('');
 2922:       return '<div>' + inner + '</div>';
 2923:     }).join('');

 1219:   // Strip C0 EXCEPT \t and \n. validate.py rejects them; stripping here means the user never gets a
 1220:   // 422 for a character they cannot see. (혻 is NOT C0 and is preserved verbatim.)
 1221:   function stripC0(t) { return t.replace(/[\x00-\x08\x0b-\x1f]/g, ''); }
 1222: 
 1223:   var BLOCK_TAGS = { DIV: 1, P: 1, LI: 1, H1: 1, H2: 1, H3: 1, H4: 1, H5: 1, H6: 1, BLOCKQUOTE: 1, PRE: 1 };
 1224: 
 1225:   function hex2(n) { return ('0' + n.toString(16)).slice(-2).toUpperCase(); }
 1226: 
 1227:   function cssColorToHex(raw) {
 1228:     var v = String(raw).trim();
 1229:     var m = v.match(/^rgba?\(\s*(\d+)\s*,\s*(\d+)\s*,\s*(\d+)/i);
 1230:     if (m) return '#' + hex2(+m[1]) + hex2(+m[2]) + hex2(+m[3]);
 1231:     m = v.match(/^#([0-9a-f]{6})$/i);
 1232:     if (m) return '#' + m[1].toUpperCase();
 1233:     m = v.match(/^#([0-9a-f]{3})$/i);
 1234:     if (m) {
 1235:       var s = m[1];
 1236:       return '#' + (s[0] + s[0] + s[1] + s[1] + s[2] + s[2]).toUpperCase();
 1237:     }
 1238:     return null; // a named colour or anything exotic: fall back to the element default
 1239:   }
 1240: 
 1241:   // The INLINE style attribute only -- never the browser's COMPUTED style, which is banned (Gate 1)
 1242:   // and would silently bake the CSS default colour into every single run.
 1243:   function colorOf(node) {
 1244:     var inline = node.style && node.style.color;
 1245:     if (inline) { var c = cssColorToHex(inline); if (c) return c; }
 1246:     if (node.tagName === 'FONT') {
 1247:       var attr = node.getAttribute('color');
 1248:       if (attr) { var f = cssColorToHex(attr); if (f) return f; }
 1249:     }
 1250:     return null;
 1251:   }
 1252: 
 1253:   /* normalizeText(root) -> [paragraph, ...] | null
 1254:    *
 1255:    * Every run carries t, size and color -- all three are REQUIRED by the schema. An un-coloured run
 1256:    * inherits fontRef -> lt1, which is WHITE, and that is the rule that stops white-on-white text.
 1257:    *
 1258:    * defBold/defItalic are the ELEMENT's flags, and they are OR-ed into every run -- the same
 1259:    * relationship defSize and defColor already have with the runs, just spelled out. The IR has no
 1260:    * element-level bold to serialize them into; run.bold is the only place they can land. A <b> or a
 1261:    * <strong> INSIDE the body (a paste, or the browser's own Ctrl+B) still bolds its own run on its
 1262:    * own, which is why turning the element flag off has to remove those tags -- see setTextFlag().
 1263:    */
 1264:   function normalizeText(root, defSize, defColor, defBold, defItalic) {
 1265:     var paras = [];
 1266:     var cur = null;
 1267: 
 1268:     function startPara() { cur = []; paras.push(cur); }
 1269:     startPara();
 1270: 
 1271:     function push(text, fmt) {
 1272:       if (!text) return;
 1273:       var run = { t: text, size: defSize, color: fmt.color || defColor };
 1274:       if (fmt.bold || defBold) run.bold = true;
 1275:       if (fmt.italic || defItalic) run.italic = true;
 1276:       var last = cur[cur.length - 1];
 1277:       // Merge adjacent runs with identical formatting.
 1278:       if (last && !!last.bold === !!run.bold && !!last.italic === !!run.italic && last.color === run.color) {
 1279:         last.t += run.t;
 1280:       } else {
 1281:         cur.push(run);
 1282:       }
 1283:     }
 1284: 
 1285:     function walk(node, fmt) {
 1286:       var kids = node.childNodes;
 1287:       for (var i = 0; i < kids.length; i++) {
 1288:         var child = kids[i];
 1289: 
 1290:         if (child.nodeType === 3) { // text
 1291:           push(stripC0(child.nodeValue), fmt);
 1292:           continue;
 1293:         }
 1294:         if (child.nodeType !== 1) continue; // comments and the rest
 1295: 
 1296:         var tag = child.tagName;
 1297: 
 1298:         if (tag === 'BR') {
 1299:           // A <br> that is the LAST child of its block is the browser's own filler -- Chrome puts
 1300:           // one in every empty contenteditable block to give it height. Emitting it would add a
 1301:           // phantom trailing line to the PPTX.
 1302:           if (child === node.lastChild) continue;
 1303:           push('\n', fmt); // style.py splits on \n and emits <a:br/>
 1304:           continue;
 1305:         }
 1306: 
 1307:         var f = { bold: fmt.bold, italic: fmt.italic, color: fmt.color };
 1308:         if (tag === 'B' || tag === 'STRONG') f.bold = true;
 1309:         if (tag === 'I' || tag === 'EM') f.italic = true;
 1310:         var c = colorOf(child);
 1311:         if (c) f.color = c;
 1312: 
 1313:         if (BLOCK_TAGS[tag]) startPara(); // a block element is a NEW PARAGRAPH
 1314:         walk(child, f);
 1315:       }
 1316:     }
 1317: 
 1318:     walk(root, { bold: false, italic: false, color: null });
 1319: 
 1320:     // Drop empty runs, then empty paragraphs -- browsers leave trailing empty <div>s behind
 1321:     // constantly.
 1322:     var out = [];
 1323:     for (var p = 0; p < paras.length; p++) {
 1324:       var runs = paras[p].filter(function (r) { return r.t !== ''; });
 1325:       if (runs.length) out.push({ runs: runs });
 1326:     }
 1327:     // If nothing survives, the caller emits "text": null -- NOT paragraphs: [], which the schema
 1328:     // rejects (minItems: 1).
 1329:     return out.length ? out : null;
 1330:   }
 1331: 
 1332:   // The title is the same walk, flattened to a plain STRING. A "\n" becomes an <a:br/> in
 1333:   // placeholder 10.
 1334:   function titleString() {
 1335:     var out = '';
 1336:     (function walk(node) {
 1337:       var kids = node.childNodes;
 1338:       for (var i = 0; i < kids.length; i++) {
 1339:         var child = kids[i];
 1340:         if (child.nodeType === 3) { out += child.nodeValue; continue; }
 1341:         if (child.nodeType !== 1) continue;
 1342:         if (child.tagName === 'BR') {
 1343:           if (child !== node.lastChild) out += '\n';
 1344:           continue;
 1345:         }
 1346:         if (BLOCK_TAGS[child.tagName] && out !== '') out += '\n';
 1347:         walk(child);
 1348:       }
 1349:     })(titleField);
 1350:     return stripC0(out).replace(/\n+$/, '');
 1351:   }

 1353:   /* [REDACTED] serializer */
 1354: 
 1355:   // Folds a 0-100 opacity PERCENT into the trailing AA byte of a #RRGGBB colour, per schema.json's
 1356:   // `color` def (#RRGGBB, or #RRGGBBAA where the trailing byte is alpha). 100 (opaque) emits no
 1357:   // byte at all -- an unmodified 6-digit colour is the common case and stays exactly what it was
 1358:   // before Phase 6, so decks with no transparency are byte-identical to what this editor emitted
 1359:   // before this feature existed.
 1360:   function withAlpha(hex6, alphaPct) {
 1361:     if (typeof alphaPct !== 'number' || alphaPct >= 100) return hex6;
 1362:     return hex6 + hex2(Math.round(clamp(alphaPct, 0, 100) / 100 * 255));
 1363:   }

 1180:     if (!el && multi.length) { clearMulti(); syncProps(); }
 1181:     if (selected === el) return;
 1182:     stopEditing(); // leaving an element always closes its text
 1183:     if (selected) selected.classList.remove('selected');
 1184:     selected = el;
 1185:     if (selected) selected.classList.add('selected');
 1186:     syncProps();
 1187:   }

 1190:    *

 1193:    * `selected`, so the property panel keeps describing whichever element was last PLAIN-clicked
 1194:    * (or shows nothing if none has been, even with a multi-selection in progress). */

 1196:   function selectionSet() {
 1197:     var set = [];

 1199:     for (var i = 0; i < multi.length; i++) {
 1200:       if (set.indexOf(multi[i]) < 0) set.push(multi[i]);
 1201:     }
 1202:     return set;
 1203:   }

 1960:       return;

 1964:       return;
 1965:     }

# Task Overview
We are adding an HTML Export feature and extending the `canvas-ir/1` schema to support hyperlinks (`link` property) so that exported HTML can have interactive links (e.g. to a separate algorithm explanation slide). 

## Requirements
1. **IR Schema Extension (`schema.json`)**:
   - Add a `"link": {"type": "string"}` property to the schema. The safest place is either at the element level (for shapes) or inside the text run. Given PPTX usually links shapes or text, adding it to the `shape` definition (or globally to all elements via `additionalProperties` or similar) is easiest. Let's add it to `shape` (rect, ellipse, triangle, textbox) as an optional `"link": {"type": "string"}`.

2. **Frontend UI (`index.html` & `editor.js`)**:
   - Add a `<label>Link <input type="url" id="p-link"></label>` to `aside#props` in `index.html`.
   - Update `editor.js` to read/write this `link` property from the element's dataset.
   - Add a "Download HTML" button in `index.html` (e.g. `<button type="button" id="b-export-html">Download HTML</button>`) next to the "Export PPT" button.

3. **HTML Export Logic (`editor.js`)**:
   - Implement the `exportHTML()` function tied to the new "Download HTML" button.
   - The export must bundle the `div#stage` contents into a standalone HTML string (including necessary CSS from `style.css` or inline styles for absolute positioning).
   - Elements that have a `link` property should be wrapped in an `<a>` tag or have an `onclick` event to navigate to the link when clicked in the exported HTML.
   - Trigger a browser download of `presentation.html`.

## Guidelines
- Follow the single-source-of-truth geometry rules in `editor.js`.
- Make sure the exported HTML visually matches the canvas (absolute positioning, correct colors, z-index).
- Adhere strictly to the write_scope. Do not touch backend python files unless necessary (they shouldn't be, as schema.json drives validation).

# Task Overview (Repair Mode: Continuation)
You are in **Repair Mode** for the Phase 6 HTML Export feature. Your previous attempt generated the diff attached below. However, the Codex Critic reviewer blocked your code for two critical security/offline-capability issues. 
Previous diff path (read it using your tools): `C:\Users\2021670\Documents\멀티 에이전트\tasks\AI_Powered_PPT\artifacts\claude-main-diff-1907888-7359.txt`

## Reviewer Feedback (BLOCK)
- `editor.js:2145-2163` clones media elements with transient `blob:` URLs assigned at `editor.js:753-756`. Images/videos will break after the editor session ends, violating the stated standalone/offline requirement. Media must be embedded, such as with data URLs.
- `editor.js:2150` places arbitrary `link` values into `href`. HTML escaping prevents attribute injection but does not block executable schemes such as `javascript:`. Restrict links to approved protocols and anchors.

## Requirements
Please generate a NEW complete patch (using the same write_scope) that includes your previous work but fixes the two issues above:
1. **Media Embedding**: Ensure that when exporting HTML, `blob:` URLs used by media elements are converted to Base64 `data:` URLs (or handle them appropriately so they work offline). You may need to fetch the blob and convert it, or store the original base64 in dataset when the user uploads it.
2. **Link Security**: Before injecting the `link` dataset property into the `<a href="...">` attribute during HTML export, sanitize the URL. Ensure it only allows `http:`, `https:`, `mailto:`, or in-page anchors (`#...`). Block or remove `javascript:`, `data:`, `vbscript:`, etc.

## Guidelines
- Write the full replacement logic. Do not assume the previous diff was applied (it was aborted). Your new diff will be applied cleanly to the original files.
- Follow the original Phase 6 instructions as well (Hyperlink schema, UI additions, and HTML export).

- `editor.js:2203-2215` and `editor.js:2269` serialize live `contenteditable` markup through `clone.outerHTML` and `titleField.innerHTML`. Rich pasted content can therefore preserve unsafe tags, event handlers, or nested `javascript:` links, bypassing `sanitizeLink()` and executing when the exported file opens. Reconstruct text from normalized/escaped data or sanitize cloned DOM with strict tag/attribute allowlists.
